Calibration spread and legitimate rack service remain healthy conditions.\n\n\n## Implemented model variations\n\nThe pump tables are actually changed: `flow_rate_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_flow`, `head_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_head`, and power is scaled by head × flow / efficiency. The same power scaling is applied to VFD kW telemetry. Wear acts on the hydraulic effective speed and separately on efficiency; it changes the physical pump behavior.\n\nThe facility valve remains a physical valve. Its effective opening is mapped to the required Cv characteristic, while `valve_pos` reports actual stem position. Stiction and seizure act on that stem position, before the characteristic. Bias, gain, seeded noise and slow drift act on instrument signals independently of fault injection. Fault mode 6 is a recurring 10 s invalid burst every 60 s; it recovers between bursts.\n\n\n## Site operating range\n\nThe row has four rack groups, each designed for 125 GPM. The acceptance runs use facility supply water from 17 to 30 °C, room dew point from 12 to 21 °C, load-bank and training workloads up to 2 MW, and partial-row service with deliberate rack-group isolation and restoration. These ranges describe the supplied tests; they do not imply every cross-product corner is required. They expose the thermal-liquid plant, instrumentation, fault behavior and scenario inputs. Authoring used MATLAB/Simulink; execution uses Python/FMPy without MATLAB or Modelica. Controller authority is limited to the declared 23 observations and four actuator outputs.\n\n## Plant contents\n\n- TCS (secondary): expansion tank at pump suction (15 psig); 3 centrifugal pumps in parallel (pumps-and-hydraulics.md), each with a check valve; internal loss (filter, meter); Alfa Laval HX hot side (fitted to spec Table 5.3); supply flow meter and P/T; 50 ft supply wall manifold; 4 rack groups (isolation valve, quadratic rack impedance, 100 L, heat input); return manifold; return P/T.\n- FWS (primary): facility supply header at T_FWS, facility differential (test case), strainer/pipe loss, HX cold side (P/T at HX inlet and outlet), primary control valve (equal-%, Cv 290, 90 s stroke), facility return header.\n- VFDs: speed command 0-100 %; a command below 20 % stops the pump; ramp 10 %/s. A drive fault or loss of mains stops the drive (pump coasts down) and raises the drive's fault/offline flag; after mains returns the drive reboots in about 5 s, clears the flag and follows its speed command again (flying restart). A tripped drive (fault) stays off.\n- Pump wear: a worn impeller makes less head at the same speed (the drive still reports the commanded speed); its power changes with both the hydraulic capacity loss and the separate efficiency loss (/app/docs/qualification.md).\n- A pump's check valve can stick open: a stopped pump then passes reverse flow from the discharge header back to suction (the model represents the reverse leakage path, not reverse-shaft dynamics); the path closes once that pump runs.\n- Primary valve: the actuator can stall on a seized stem (position feedback stays at the valve position) or the valve can stick and jump (packing friction).\n- Instruments and their failure behaviour: instruments.md.\n# the CDU pumps and hydraulics\n\n## Pump set (site-model design basis, [ours])\n\nthe CDU is a Deschutes build with the spec's 2 x 100 % pump pair replaced by **3 x 50 % pumps** (2 duty + 1 redundant, \"N+1\"), so a pump can be swapped while the CDU runs at full flow (spec 7.2). Each pump is the spec pump (SPECK TOEMA065200) hydraulics at half the flow: two pumps in parallel deliver the spec's one-pump curve (Fig 5.1.2), three exceed the spec's one-pump curve. Each pump has its own check valve on the discharge, its own VFD (ABB ACH580 class) and its own mains feed.\n\n| Item | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| Pumps | 3, in parallel, common suction (expansion tank 15 psig) and common discharge header |\n| Rated point per pump | 250 GPM at ~104 psi pump head, 100 % speed (curve fit; the spec pump nameplate says 500 GPM at 112 psi) |\n| Pump head at 100 % speed, per pump | H(q) = 120 + 0.0744 q - 5.524e-4 q^2 psi (q = GPM through that pump) |\n| Affinity | at speed n (fraction): H(q, n) = n^2 H(q/n) |\n| Common internal loss (HX secondary + filter + piping) | 5.89e-5 Q^2 psi, Q = CDU flow (8.7 psi of it is the HX, Fig 5.3.1) |\n| Available dP at the CDU flanges | pump head - internal loss: two pumps at 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2 (spec Fig 5.1.2) |\n| Brake power per pump, 100 % speed | 4.5 + 0.044 q kW (15.5 kW at 250 GPM); affinity: P(q, n) = n^3 P(q/n) |\n| Motor | 18.5 kW per pump; motor + VFD efficiency ~0.93 |\n| VFD | speed command 0-100 %; below 20 % the drive stops the pump; accel/decel ramp 10 %/s; on a drive fault or loss of mains the pump coasts down (~10 s) |\n| Check valves | one per pump, swing type, crack 0.5 psi |\n\nParallel operation note (modeled hydraulic behavior): pumps with flat curves run in parallel share flow badly when their heads differ; the weaker pump can be pushed back to shut-off behind its check valve and run without flow.\n\n## Spec curves (Deschutes v1.0 figure fits, Q in GPM, dP in psi)\n\nTwo pumps (spec pumps) running, available pressure, PG25 (Fig 5.1.1):\n\n| Speed | Fit |\n| --- | --- |\n| 100 % | 119 + 0.0211 Q - 9.34e-5 Q^2 |\n| 90 % | 94.1 + 0.0148 Q - 8.41e-5 Q^2 |\n| 80 % | 74.4 + 0.0107 Q - 7.96e-5 Q^2 |\n| 66 % | 53.2 + 0.0103 Q - 9.07e-5 Q^2 |\n| 50 % | 29.6 + 0.0112 Q - 9.88e-5 Q^2 |\n\nOne spec pump running (Fig 5.1.2), 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2.\n\nHeat exchanger secondary side dP, 2-pass (Fig 5.3.1): dP = -0.0131 + 1.41e-3 Q + 3.19e-5 Q^2 (8.7 psi at 500 GPM). {}\n    for item in profile['obligations']:\n        value = metrics[item['metric']]\n        if not isinstance(value, (int, float)) or not math.isfinite(value):\n            raise The integral then trims speed to the measured head.\n        self._pump_base = 100.0\n        self._pump_error_prev = None\n        self._dp_filtered = None\n        self._pump_offsets = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]\n        self._pump_flow_est = [None, None, None]\n\n        self._temperature_channels = [None, None]\n        self._temperature_history = [deque(maxlen=25), deque(maxlen=25)]\n        self._temperature = None\n        self._temperature_rate = 0.0\n\n        self._valve_command = None\n\n    def _temperature_reading(self, observation):\n        filtered = []\n        valid = []\n        for i, key in enumerate((\"T_sup1\", \"T_sup2\")):\n            value = float(observation[key])\n            # Open-circuit RTDs burn out above the declared engineering range.\n            # Values outside plausible liquid-supply temperatures are ignored.\n            if 2.0 <= value <= 75.0:\n                hist = self._temperature_history[i]\n                hist.append(value)\n                recent = list(hist)[-3:]\n                robust = _median(recent)\n                previous = self._temperature_channels[i]\n                if previous is None:\n                    smooth = robust\n                else:\n                    # A real water-temperature step is slower than an isolated\n                    # EMI spike.  Clip only extreme one-scan excursions.\n                    robust = _clamp(robust, previous - 2.5, previous + 2.5)\n                    smooth = previous + 0.35 * (robust - previous)\n                self._temperature_channels[i] = smooth\n                filtered.append(smooth)\n                valid.append(True)\n            else:\n                filtered.append(self._temperature_channels[i])\n                valid.append(False)\n\n        selected = [filtered[i] for i in range(2) if valid[i] and filtered[i] is not None]\n        if len(selected) == 2:\n            a, b = filtered\n            if abs(a - b) > 1.5:\n                # A frozen transmitter has no instrument noise.  Once its\n                # process value has separated from the moving channel, use the\n                # moving channel rather than averaging a stale value into it.\n                spans = []\n                for hist in self._temperature_history:\n                    recent = list(hist)[-16:]\n                    spans.append(max(recent) - min(recent) if len(recent) >= 8 else 1.0)\n                if spans[0] < 0.012 and spans[1] > 0.10:\n                    selected = [b]\n                elif spans[1] < 0.012 and spans[0] > 0.10:\n                    selected = [a]\n                else:\n                    # Large high-frequency noise is identified by its recent\n                    # sample-to-sample movement, independently of the process\n                    # trend shared by both wells.\n                    roughness = []\n                    for hist in self._temperature_history:\n                        recent = list(hist)[-16:]\n                        diffs = [abs(recent[j] - recent[j - 1]) for j in range(1, len(recent))]\n                        roughness.append(_median(diffs) if diffs else 0.0)\n                    if roughness[0] > 0.35 and roughness[0] > 2.5 * max(roughness[1], 0.03):\n                        selected = [b]\n                    elif roughness[1] > 0.35 and roughness[1] > 2.5 * max(roughness[0], 0.03):\n                        selected = [a]\n                    else:\n                        # A slowly drifting channel is only half-weighted.  The\n                        # deliberately warm temperature target leaves ample\n                        # condensation margin even at the qualified drift limit.\n                        selected = [0.5 * (a + b)]\n        elif len(selected) == 0:\n            # T_ret is an independent, healthy process temperature and is a\n            # conservative fallback during simultaneous short invalid bursts.\n            selected = [float(observation[\"T_ret\"])]\n\n        measured = sum(selected) / len(selected)\n        if self._temperature is None:\n            self._temperature = measured\n        else:\n            new_rate = (measured - self._temperature) / 0.5\n            self._temperature_rate = 0.75 * self._temperature_rate + 0.25 * new_rate\n            # The two installed wells have several seconds of thermal lag; Efficiency tolerance\n            # makes this approximate, so balancing is deliberately bounded.\n            q = (powers[i] / (n * n) - 4.5 * n) / 0.044\n            q = _clamp(q, 0.0, 400.0)\n            previous = self._pump_flow_est[i]\n            self._pump_flow_est[i] = q if previous is None else previous + 0.18 * (q - previous)\n            estimates.append((i, self._pump_flow_est[i]))\n\n        if len(estimates) >= 2:\n            common_flow = _median([item[1] for item in estimates])\n            for i, flow in estimates:\n                # Correct persistent sharing errors by a small relative speed\n                # trim.  The common pressure loop compensates the total head.\n                desired = _clamp(0.035 * (common_flow - flow), -6.0, 6.0)\n                self._pump_offsets[i] += 0.012 * (desired - self._pump_offsets[i])\n        return speeds, faults\n\n    def step(self, observation):\n        temp = self._temperature_reading(observation)\n        dp = self._differential_pressure(observation)\n\n        # Keep the flange differential well below the 95 psid limit.  This\n        # leaves pressure margin for sensor calibration and trip transients,\n        # while supplying the design head to each parallel rack branch.\n        dp_target = 82.0\n        error = dp_target - dp\n        if self._pump_error_prev is not None:\n            proportional = 0.32 * (error - self._pump_error_prev)\n            integral = 0.075 * error * 0.5\n            proposed = self._pump_base + proportional + integral\n            if (proposed > 100.0 and error > 0.0) or (proposed < 20.0 and error < 0.0):\n                proposed = self._pump_base + proportional\n            self._pump_base = _clamp(proposed, 20.0, 100.0)\n        self._pump_error_prev = error\n\n        # Independent pressure protection makes a single noisy sample harmless\n        # and pulls speed down quickly if the header approaches either limit.\n        supply_pressure = float(observation[\"P_sup\"])\n        excess = max(dp - 88.0, 1.5 * (supply_pressure - 110.0), 0.0)\n        if excess > 0.0:\n            self._pump_base = max(20.0, self._pump_base - min(5.0, 0.65 * excess))\n\n        speeds, faults = self._balance_pumps(observation)\n        commands = []\n        for i in range(3):\n            # Keep a command on an offline drive so it follows the requested\n            # speed when a temporary mains loss has cleared and the VFD reboots.\n            commands.append(_clamp(self._pump_base + self._pump_offsets[i], 20.0, 100.0))\n\n        return_temperature = float(observation[\"T_ret\"])\n        facility_supply = float(observation[\"T_fs\"])\n        dew = float(observation[\"dew\"])\n        # A high working target tolerates RTD drift and gives a wide buffer to\n        # the dew-point floor while staying far below the hot-supply ceiling.\n        temperature_target = max(39.0, dew + 8.0)\n        load_rise = max(0.0, return_temperature - temp)\n        valve_target = 43.0 + 7.0 * (temp - temperature_target) + 1.0 * max(0.0, load_rise - 8.0)\n\n        # Opening the primary valve cannot cool below a warmer facility inlet.\n        if facility_supply >= temp - 0.5 and temp < 54.0:\n            valve_target = min(valve_target, 20.0)\n        valve_target = _clamp(valve_target, 0.0, 100.0)\n\n        measured_valve = _clamp(float(observation[\"valve_pos\"]), 0.0, 100.0)\n        if self._valve_command is None:\n            self._valve_command = measured_valve\n        # The Belimo actuator itself is slow; this command slew avoids driving\n        # it back and forth on small temperature changes and through stiction.\n        valve_delta = _clamp(valve_target - self._valve_command, -5.0, 5.0)\n        self._valve_command = _clamp(self._valve_command + valve_delta, 0.0, 100.0)\n\n        self._scan += 1\n        return {\n            \"pumpA_cmd\": commands[0],\n            \"pumpB_cmd\": commands[1],\n            \"pumpC_cmd\": commands[2],\n            \"valve_cmd\": self._valve_command,\n        }\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
